AMD is behaving as a clean momentum leader on the semis-led tape; it remains a high-quality watchlist name — the issue was order mechanics, **not** the name. No park.
- **TSM** flipped from a 06-29 false-break loss to a +1.92% TP win on the green tape — textbook regime-dependence (name behaves with the tape, not a structural defect). **MU** finally produced its first live signal since the 06-24 blowout and won (+1.78% to flatten) — the post-earnings gap has settled; keep. **TSLA** now **4W0L** (+$375 14d), still the franchise earner. **AAPL** rebounded (+1.29%) — the opposite of its 06-29 open-fade, i.e. regime not name.

### What worked / what didn't
- **Worked — directional-with-the-tape paid a third straight session.** On the risk-on continuation the breakout/MA setups followed through: 2 clean TPs (SE, MSFT), 3 green EOD flattens, one franchise-adjacent BOTH win (GOOGL). Same green-tape behavior as 06-29/06-30 — reinforces that **tape direction is the discriminator** (the #1 strategy lever), not any per-trade score.
- **Worked — every risk/measurement fix held.** IMP-002 (no-overnight: 0 open on Alpaca, retry-until-confirmed flatten fired exactly as designed), IMP-003/IMP-005 (day gross == broker equity move to the penny), IMP-004/IMP-006 (low-conf MA book carried the day: SE/MSFT/AAPL/AMZN all conf 60–65 and all green — the "raise the MA floor" candidate stays refuted). IMP-008 caused **no false skips** (all 6 real entries filled; fills ≤0.12% off signal).
- **Didn't — one false-breakout STOP (ENPH) was the whole loss**, exactly the STOP/false-breakout bucket (PF 0.01) that is the entire all-time leak. No one-day fix: every *per-trade* discriminator (confidence IMP-004, volume 06-26, extension IMP-007) is refuted; the lever is the market-regime gate (deliberate replay build, not a post-close tweak).
- **The real defect is a MISSED entry, not a loss: NVDA's bracket 422** — the exact down-gap twin of the AMD up-gap 422 that IMP-008 was built for yesterday. IMP-008 only guarded the *up* direction; NVDA gapped *down* so the stop leg went above the live price and Alpaca rejected the whole bracket. Highest-impact, capital-relevant, data-justified issue today → IMP-009.

### Lessons & improvement candidates (ranked)
1. **(ACTED → IMP-009) Symmetric stale-signal / gap guard.** IMP-008 skipped entries where the live price ran > `MAX_ENTRY_SLIPPAGE_PCT` (1.0%) **above** the signal close (up-gap → TP 422). NVDA today is the **mirror**: it gapped **down** ~1.5% between signal and submission, so the stop (anchored ~1.5% below the signal close) landed at/above the live 195.02 → `stop_loss.stop_price must be <= base_price - 0.01` 422 → entry silently lost (no `signals`/`trades` row, invisible to analytics — same as AMD 06-30). Fix: the guard now skips when the live price moves > 1.0% from the signal close in **either** direction (`abs(slip) > MAX_ENTRY_SLIPPAGE_PCT`), with a direction-aware `stale_signal_gap_up/down` reason logged. Also catches the shallower (1.0–1.5%) down-gap that Alpaca *accepts* but that compresses the stop to a hair-trigger while the breakout premise has already failed (price back below the level). Recorded fills are ≤0.5% off the signal both ways, so no false skips. **A NEW skip (tightening) — NO risk limit widened** (paper endpoint, MAX_RISK_PCT 2.0, DAILY_LOSS_HALT_PCT 8.0, MAX_CONCURRENT 3, no-overnight all unchanged).
2. **(STANDING #1 lever) Intraday market-regime entry gate** — the real strategy work, unchanged. ENPH's false-break STOP today (the only loss) is the STOP bucket the gate targets; but it needs the deliberate multi-run SPY/QQQ-VWAP replay build, not a one-day change. Every per-trade discriminator remains refuted.
3. **(Note, no action)** Missed/rejected entries still produce no `signals` row, so they're invisible to the report/analytics — only `bot.log` shows them (IMP-009 now logs a clean `ENTRY SKIPPED … stale_signal_gap_down` line for the class it prevents, but a genuine broker-side 422 for another reason would still be log-only). If rejections become frequent, persist skipped attempts. Backlog note, unchanged from 06-30.

- Circuit breaker NOT tripped (−1.75% << −8.0% halt). Service active all session, no errors. **0 open positions on Alpaca**, no overnight carry (all 3 non-stop names EOD-flattened 15:57 ET; IMP-002 held).
- **NFP-day open-fade** — exactly the whipsaw/open-fade regime the pre-market research flagged into the payrolls print. All 4 entries in the first ~2h (09:30–11:45 ET); nothing followed through.

### Trade-by-trade review
- **GOOGL** #111 — MA, conf 62.2. In 09:30:25 @**360.84** (real fill; DB had recorded the plan price 361.19 — see IMP-010), out 11:44:52 @355.76 (**STOP**), −$35.58 (−1.41%). Opened at the bell, faded straight down to the ~1.5% stop over 2h. MA-only open-fade, no breakout level. Root cause: **regime (open-fade), not stop placement** (stop was the normal ATR/1.5%-floor distance).
- **SE** #112 — BOTH, conf 73.2, broke_level 103.275. In 09:31:46 (submitted) but **filled 09:35:10 @104.73** (a ~3.4-min market-fill lag on the fast open → filled **1.40% above** the broken level), out 15:57:21 @102.88 (**EOD_FLATTEN**), **−$99.72 (−1.76%, 67% of the day's loss)**. Classic **false breakout**: reversed back below 103.275 and bled all session, never hitting the 102.05 stop → carried to the flatten. Root cause: **false breakout + extended/late fill** (chased 1.4% above the level; the >1.0%-extension bucket is the worst all-time, PF 0.09).
- **CRM** #113 — MA, conf 60.9. In 09:41:45 @165.98, out 15:57:21 @165.22 (**EOD_FLATTEN**), −$12.94 (−0.46%). Low-conf MA drift; never trended, small controlled bleed to the flatten. Root cause: **no-follow-through regime**.
- **BAC** #114 — MA, conf 62.9. In 11:44:58 @58.67 (entered the slot GOOGL vacated when it stopped 11:44:52), out 15:57:22 @58.67 (**EOD_FLATTEN**), **$0.00 scratch**. Verified against Alpaca: buy and sell both filled 58.67. Root cause: **dead/no-follow-through regime** (flat all afternoon).

### What worked / what didn't
- **Worked — every risk/measurement invariant held.** No overnight carry (0 on Alpaca), retry-until-confirmed flatten fired at 15:57, circuit breaker correctly dormant at −1.75%, day gross tied to the broker equity move to the penny. Pre-market call was right: it flagged NFP open-fade risk and did NOT add names into the print — the loss was regime, not a bad watchlist.
- **Didn't — the tape faded the open and nothing trended.** 3 of 4 entries were the STOP/false-breakout-and-drift family the all-time books already indict (STOP bucket PF 0.01; EOD_FLATTEN drift on names that never followed through). SE's extended 1.4%-above-level chase was the single worst trade — the >1.0%-extension bucket (PF 0.09) biting again, though a hard extension cap stays **refuted** (IMP-007: the tight ≤0.5% bucket is also net-negative; extension doesn't cleanly discriminate).
- **The real defect found today is a MEASUREMENT bug, not a loss: GOOGL's stored `entry_price` was the stale plan/signal price (361.19), not the real bracket fill (360.84).** Its P&L (−35.58) was already computed off the real fill, so the row *didn't reconcile* (entry×exit×qty ≠ realized_pl) and the by-entry-extension analytic (IMP-007) would mis-measure every slipped STOP/TP breakout. Highest-impact, capital-safe, data-justified issue today → **IMP-010**.

### Lessons & improvement candidates (ranked)
1. **(ACTED → IMP-010) STOP/TP path now corrects `entry_price` to the real bracket fill.** `exits.build_exit_record` already prices P&L off the parent order's `filled_avg_price`, but `logbook.record_exit` forwarded only exit_price/pl/reason to `update_trade_exit` — never the real entry — so the `trades.entry_price` column kept the plan price for STOP/TP exits (GOOGL 361.19 vs fill 360.84). This is the **STOP/TP analog of IMP-005** (which corrected only the EOD_FLATTEN path); it closes the last hole in the fill-accuracy program (IMP-003 exit-flatten, IMP-005 entry-flatten, now IMP-010 entry STOP/TP). Fix: `record_exit` forwards `entry_price=exit_record.get("entry_price")`. Pure measurement-integrity — makes rows reconcile and the extension analytic truthful. **NO entry logic, NO sizing, NO stop, NO risk limit touched** (paper endpoint, MAX_RISK_PCT 2.0, DAILY_LOSS_HALT_PCT 8.0, MAX_CONCURRENT 3, no-overnight all unchanged). Also backfilled today's GOOGL row (361.19→360.8429; P&L unchanged, now reconciles).
2. **(STANDING #1 lever) Intraday market-regime entry gate** — unchanged, still the real strategy work. Today (NFP open-fade, 3/4 no-follow-through) is textbook for it: `regime_ok=True` fired on all 4 despite a choppy fade tape, so the current regime flag does NOT filter no-follow-through days. Needs the deliberate multi-run SPY/QQQ-VWAP/breadth replay build, not a one-day tweak. Every *per-trade* discriminator (confidence IMP-004, volume 06-26, extension IMP-007) remains refuted; the lever is market-level.
3. **(Watch, no action) SE's ~3.4-min market-order fill lag** on the fast open pushed the fill 1.40% above the broken level. The IMP-008/009 stale-signal guard checks live-vs-signal-close **at submission**, not fill latency after submission, so it didn't catch this. One occurrence, likely a paper-fill/data artifact — do not act; re-flag only if a pattern of multi-minute market fills recurs (would inflate realized entry-extension vs plan).

### Notes for pre-market research
- **Regime, not names.** Today's −$148 was an NFP-day open-fade with no follow-through — every loser behaved with the tape, none is a name defect. No park triggers matured. All 4 traded names (GOOGL/SE/CRM/BAC) are fine watchlist members.
- **SE** — the day's worst (−$99.72): a genuine BOTH breakout (conf 73) that filled 1.4% above the level (~3.4-min fill lag), reversed below it, and bled to the flatten. Recurring false-breakout-then-drift mode; behaves with the regime, **no park**. Watch whether SE keeps filling late on fast opens.
- **GOOGL** — MA open-fade stop (−$35.58). Park watch already CLOSED (07-01 win); keep both GOOG and GOOGL. No new concern beyond regime.
- **CRM / BAC** — low-conf MA, no follow-through (−$12.94 / scratch). Nothing name-specific; dead-tape drift/flat.
- **Market CLOSED Fri 07-03; next session Mon 07-06.** Review how the NFP print + long-weekend tape resolved: if the fade regime persists, megacap/MA open-fades (today's GOOGL, prior AAPL) stay the risk — the #1 strategy-lever regime gate. If chip/AI leadership resumes, on-list semis are the vehicles. Equity **$8,301.08 (−17.0%)**, **$801 above** the −25% ($7,500) flag — cushion trimmed by one fade day but intact; protect it.

### Lessons & improvement candidates (ranked)
1. **(ACTED → IMP-012, measurement-only) Today is a COUNTEREXAMPLE to the naive skip-bearish market-regime gate — and cross-proxy analysis shows its edge is proxy-dependent.** The only winner (META +$85.74 TP) was tagged **BEARISH** under both SPY and QQQ, while the worst loser (AMZN −$43.97 STOP) was tagged **BULLISH**. So on today's tape skip-bearish would have **removed +$46.73 of net-positive P&L and kept −$54.95** — actively harmful. Extended `scripts/regime_analysis.py` to measure the regime under **two proxies (SPY + QQQ, EMA9)** side by side + added a `--since YYYY-MM-DD` window and a pure `analytics.regime_proxy_agreement` helper (**no engine/sizing/risk change**). **Result on the post-06-15 live regime:** under **SPY** bullish PF 1.43 vs bearish PF 0.99 (skip-bearish removes only −$2.57 — barely helps), but under **QQQ** bearish is *profitable* (PF 1.22, +$79.14) so skip-bearish would REMOVE +$79.14 — and the two proxies **disagree on 18% of trades (51/62 agree)**. This **tempers IMP-011's SPY-only optimism** (which read bullish 1.45 vs bearish 0.98): the naive EMA9 gate's edge does NOT survive the proxy swap, so it is **NOT ready to ship**. Measurement-first (IMP-004/006/007 pattern).
2. **(STANDING #1 lever) Intraday market-regime entry gate.** Next steps refined by today: the raw "skip-bearish under SPY-EMA9" gate is refuted as proxy-fragile — a real gate needs (a) a regime definition robust across proxies (test VWAP and "skip first N min on a red open" next, not just EMA), (b) a bigger post-06-15 bearish sample, (c) replay validation, before any engine tightening (long-only when bullish). Re-run `scripts.regime_analysis --since 2026-06-15` each review and watch whether SPY/QQQ converge.
3. **(Note, no action) Same-day re-entry after a stop** (AMZN today): 30-min cooldown allowed it after 3h. Single small instance (−$10.98); park the idea of a longer/loss-aware re-entry cooldown until a real recurring cost appears — do not overfit one AMZN.

2. **Intraday market-regime / breakout-quality entry gate (standing #1 lever)** — the residual leak (QCOM open-fade) is exactly this. ### What worked / what didn't
- **Worked: the VWAP gate (IMP-022).** 43 vetoes, including ABNB 12× at +0.56–0.73% above VWAP and TSLA 14× at up to +0.89%. It is still the one shipped filter that earns its place.

  | range / 1R | n | net | win% | PF |

### Lessons & improvement candidates (ranked)
1. **[SHIPPED IMP-033 — capital protection] The EOD flatten has failed its FIRST pass on 10 of the last 10 sessions, and today it left the bot long for 89 seconds past its own deadline.** Alpaca's own order record for today: stop legs cancelled **19:55:23.596Z**, limit legs only **19:55:26.706Z** — **3.1 seconds later**. `flatten_all` fired `close_position()` at 19:55:23.6, all three were rejected `held_for_orders`, and — because the rejection was swallowed by a bare `except: pass` — **pass 1 submitted ZERO liquidation orders** and burned a full 60s poll. Pass 2 at 19:56:25 did submit, but the engine re-checked positions ~1s later while the market sells were still in flight (fills landed 19:56:26.9–19:56:29.2), producing a **second** spurious "incomplete". Log evidence: every session 07-31 → 08-13 failed pass 1, and **6 of 10 needed a second retry**, i.e. the bot was still holding stock at ~15:57 with five minutes of runway to the close. IMP-002's retry is a *survival* net, not a fix. **Fixed by making each phase wait, bounded, for the async broker state it depends on** — shares released before liquidating, flat before returning — plus logging rejections instead of hiding them. Pure execution plumbing: **no entry logic, no signal, no sizing, no stop, no exit geometry, no risk limit touched**, so IMP-031 keeps its clean read.
2. **★ THE STRATEGY VERDICT, stated plainly: this system has no demonstrated edge, and today's work closed the last cheap escape route.** 240 trades, PF 0.61, −22.4% of the account. Post-gate — the *best* version of the bot — 51 trades at **−$4.21 expectancy**. Refuted discriminators now number eight: confidence (IMP-004), volume (06-26), extension (IMP-007), time-of-day (IMP-016), index-EMA regime (IMP-015), opening-range blackout (IMP-030), never-green time-stop (IMP-032), break-even-trigger sweep (IMP-032). **Two more died today** (both measured, both recorded so they are never re-proposed): **(a) stop-distance / `MIN_STOP_PCT`-floor binding is NOT a discriminator** — all-time floor-bound n=167 PF 0.63 vs ATR-driven n=73 PF 0.56; post-gate 0.61 vs 0.60. Flat. **(b) A time-conditioned "scratch if MFE hasn't reached X·R by T minutes" rule is refuted across a 42-cell grid** (X ∈ 0.15–0.5R, T ∈ 15–120min): **every non-degenerate cell is NEGATIVE** (e.g. 0.25R@45min −$36.15, 0.30R@30min −$88.72). The only positive cells are the top-left corner (0.5R@15min **+$176**, 0.4R@15min +$122) — which cut ~45 of 51 trades and "work" purely by removing exposure. **A rule whose optimum is "stop trading" is not an exit rule; it is the no-edge result in disguise.** The honest reading of the geometry buckets above is the same: the bot profits when its logic can't act and loses when it can — which is what a zero-edge entry plus a −1R tail looks like. **The formal strategy review is a human decision and it is now overdue; the account is below the line again.**
3. **[NOT SHIPPED — requires human approval, logged in todo.md] Post-gate stop trades held to the 15:55 flatten instead: −$273.26 vs −$386.20 actual, a +$112.94 delta (CRM alone +$115.32).** Direction of travel is real but it is a **risk-widening** change — it requires eating GOOGL #217 at −$116.77 and ENPH #200 at −$73.08 versus −$31.13/−$43.68 actual, i.e. an unbounded intraday tail in exchange for a bounded one. **Not mine to take: `MAX_RISK_PCT`, the stop, and every loss limit stay exactly where they are.** Proposed in `todo.md` for the human, with the tail cost stated.
4. **[NOT SHIPPED — noise] Any exit-geometry parameter change.** IMP-032's verdict stands and today reinforces it: the post-gate noise budget is **$248** against a book whose whole post-gate P&L is −$215. **51 trades and IEX 1-min bars cannot resolve an exit change**, and today is IMP-031's second live session with n=0 decisive cases. Do not stack.
5. **[NOT SHIPPED — measured, no defect] The four Alpaca 504s.** `consider_entries` fails last in `tick()`, after `manage_exits` and `manage_stops`, so nothing risk-bearing was skipped. Cost: four entry cycles in a 12-minute window during which the VWAP gate was vetoing everything anyway. No change warranted.

Both phases together are 33s, inside one 60s poll and the 15:55→16:00 runway. **Pure execution plumbing: no entry logic, no signal, no sizing, no stop, no exit geometry, no risk limit touched — so IMP-031 keeps its clean read.**

Log-derived MFE ≈ **435.39 (+0.91R)**, so it gave back ~0.5R from the peak — that is the trail's designed cost, not a defect. **Root cause of the win: exit machinery, not entry selection.** Slippage on the stop-market exit was **$0.04/share** (432.15 stop → 432.11 fill) — negligible.

**#260 NVDA — SCRATCH +$0.05 (+0.002%), and the most important trade of the day.** Entry 09:56:30 @ **226.201** (conf 62.48, MA), qty 10, stop 222.90 (**1R = 3.301 = 1.459%**), TP 231.38. Exit **STOP 13:14:57 @ 226.206** — a break-even stop-out.
- ★ **This is IMP-031's FIRST DECISIVE CASE in its entire life, and it matched the pre-registration exactly.** IMP-031 (live since 08-12, `n=0` decisive cases through 08-14) made the break-even stage test `max(live, peak_high_since_entry)` instead of the 60s live point-sample, and pre-registered its own narrow bound: *it can only change an outcome when `peak ≥ +0.5R AND live < +0.5R`.* At the **11:55:16** poll the log records `live 227.75, peak 227.87`. The trigger is `226.201 + 0.5 × 3.301 =` **227.8515**. **Live 227.75 was BELOW it; only the bar-high 227.87 cleared it.** Break-even armed, stop moved 222.90 → 226.20.
- **Counterfactual, and it is decisive.** Without IMP-031 the stop stays at **222.90** all day. NVDA's post-11:55 tape never returned to the trigger — session high after that bar was **227.749** and it bled steadily to an afternoon low of **224.86**, i.e. **$1.96 above the original stop, which was never touched.** So the trade survives to the EOD flatten and closes at ≈**225.04** → **10 × (225.04 − 226.201) = −$11.61**. **IMP-031 was worth +$11.66 on this trade.**
- **The trail did not silently do this instead** — had any live sample cleared 227.8515 the trail stage (which reads live by design) would have moved the stop to ≈`227.92 − 1.6505 =` **226.27**. The log records **no second NVDA stop raise all afternoon**. The absence is the evidence: the live tape was never sampled above the trigger, so only the bar-high path could have armed anything.
- **Without IMP-031 today is −$6.20, a losing day. IMP-031 flipped the sign.** ⚠️ **Stated with its caveat: n=1, worth $11.66, inside the standing $248 noise budget. This does not validate the mechanism — it is one pre-registered case resolving in favour. Do not re-tune the exit layer on it.**

**#259 QQQ — LOSS −$7.83 (−0.36%), and structurally unwinnable.** Entry 09:46:56 @ **732.0333** (conf 60.26, MA), qty 3, stop 721.06, TP 748.51. Held **6h09m** to **EOD_FLATTEN 15:56:03 @ 729.4233**.
- **Root cause is NOT signal quality, stop placement, entry timing, slippage or a bug — it is the known range-vs-1R mismatch.** Stop distance **1R = 10.973 = 1.499%**, which is the **`MIN_STOP_PCT` 1.5% floor**, not an ATR-derived level. QQQ's entire session range was **734.58 − 729.27 = 5.31** → **range / 1R = 0.48**. **The whole day's range was less than half the stop distance**; the TP sat **+16.48** away. Neither bracket leg was physically reachable from the first second of the trade. MFE **+0.23R**, MAE **−0.25R** — the trade never had a decision to make.
- This is the **7th consecutive post-gate SPY/QQQ trade to exit EOD_FLATTEN** (now ~−$14.87 net across them). Today's 0.48 is less extreme than SPY's 0.30 on 08-14, but it is the same defect. **It remains engine stop geometry owned by the weekly review, and explicitly NOT a watchlist action** — recorded here for the fourth time rather than acted on unilaterally.

### What worked / what didn't
- ✅ **The exit layer did 100% of the work, and it is now the only part of this bot with demonstrated value.** TSM's trail banked +0.41R; NVDA's break-even converted a −$11.61 EOD bleed into a $0.05 scratch. **Both winners were manufactured by exits. Zero came from entry selection.**
- ✅ **IMP-035 (weekly, shipped 08-15) PASSES its pre-registered condition, which was a NULL result.** All three fills scored **60.26 / 62.48 / 62.65** and every one sized at the **0.5% risk floor** — identical to what the old `(60,0.5) (70,1.0) (80,1.5) (90,2.0)` ladder would have granted, since all three sit in the `[60,70)` band. **Sizing and trade selection were unchanged, which is exactly the pass condition.** The alarm it ships to catch (confidence drifting into the upper bands) did not fire. `scripts.check_sizing_ladder` ALL GREEN. Sizing was risk-driven for QQQ (qty 3) and TSM (qty 5); **NVDA was notional-capped at qty 10 against a risk-implied 11.2** — the cap, not the ladder, bound it.
- ✅ **IMP-034 got its first clean live read.** The EOD flatten ran **one pass, no rejection, no false "incomplete"**: liquidation created 19:55:59.453Z, submitted 19:55:59.487Z, **filled 19:56:02.353Z (2.87s)**, book verifiably flat. Contrast 08-14, which logged the spurious *"EOD flatten incomplete — 3 position(s) still open"*. **Weak evidence (single position, an easy 2.87s fill well inside the 25s budget) but it is the right sign.**
- ✅ **The VWAP gate earned its keep**, refusing 5 stretched entries (AMD three times as it ran +0.68% → +0.32% over VWAP; XOM twice). AMD and XOM were the two names the gate blocked and neither was given away cheaply.
- ❌ **The entry signal still has no demonstrated edge, and today does not challenge that verdict.** The one trade allowed to express a directional view for a full session (QQQ, 6h09m) **lost**. The two "wins" were an exit-manufactured scratch and a trailed 0.41R. **A 66.7% win rate on 3 trades with a 0.85 payoff is noise, not a signal.** All-time remains **247 closed, PF ~0.60, expectancy negative**.
- ❌ **88% of the intraday peak was returned** ($45.98 at 12:00 ET → $5.46 at the close). All three positions were opened in the first 47 minutes and the book then had nothing to do but decay from noon; QQQ alone bled the whole afternoon in a position that could not reach an exit.
- ❌ **Observability defect found while gathering evidence (no trade impact, recorded so the next run does not trip on it).** The unit redirects `StandardOutput`/`StandardError` to `/var/log/ustradewisbot/bot.log`, so **`journalctl -u ustradewisbot.service` returns only 5 systemd lines for the whole session and none of the bot's own output** — the routine's prescribed evidence command is effectively dead. Worse, **logrotate fires at 17:00 UTC = 13:00 ET, mid-session**, so every trading day is split across two files: today's entries and stop-raises are in `bot.log.1` while TSM's exit, NVDA's exit and the flatten are in `bot.log`. A future run reading only `journalctl` could wrongly conclude the bot did nothing. **Read `/var/log/ustradewisbot/bot.log` AND `bot.log.1`.**

### Lessons & improvement candidates
1. **(Shipped today, as housekeeping) Commit IMP-034.** It has been running **unversioned** since the 08-15 restart. See the improvement log — this was explicitly assigned to today by IMP-035's entry, it is the bot's hardest invariant (no overnight holds), and a `git checkout` or redeploy would silently revert it.
2. **★ Let IMP-035 and IMP-031 run. Do NOT ship a strategy change.** Today is IMP-035's **first live session** (Monday rule) and IMP-031's **first decisive case**. Both resolved in favour on `n=1`. Stacking a change now destroys the attribution on two open reads at once.
3. **The range-vs-1R mismatch is now the best-evidenced structural defect the bot has**, at 7 consecutive post-gate SPY/QQQ EOD_FLATTENs and readings of 0.30 and 0.48. **It is a weekly/engine question** (the honest fix is an ATR-relative or instrument-aware stop floor, or declining the entry when `session_ATR / 1R` is below some bound — a genuine entry-side filter, not a `MIN_STOP_PCT` loosening, which would be risk-widening and is NOT authorised).
4. **The breakout leg has now contributed 0.0000 to four consecutive sessions' fills.** IMP-035 deliberately declined to remove it from the scorer (removal risks a confidence-scale shift). That reasoning still holds. **Keep watching, do not tidy.**
5. **Do not read today as a turnaround.** +$5.46 on 3 trades, of which +$11.66 is attributable to one exit mechanism firing once. The strategy verdict from the weekly (grade D, no demonstrated edge) is **unchanged**.

**#262 AAPL — WIN +$27.02 (+0.71R), and the exit layer earned most of it.** Bought **8 minutes after the open**, the earliest entry of the three and the only one before the day's high was in. Session 307.85 open / **311.48 high (10:41)** / 305.74 low / 310.16 close; the trade was green on **97% of its 380 minutes** and its MAE never exceeded −0.20R. **The trail ratcheted six times — 301.96 → 306.70 → 307.23 → 307.57 → 308.07 → 308.48 → 308.88** (10:04 → 10:36) — converting a −1.00R exposure into a locked **+0.46R** by 10:36, and it was never hit: the post-12:00 low was **309.02 (+0.49R)**. Exit at the flatten, **+0.71R**, giving back 0.30R from the MFE. That giveback is the trail's designed cost, not a defect. **The take-profit at 313.45 (+1.42R) was 1.97 above the entire session high — unreachable from the first second.**
- ⚠️ **One asymmetry found, and it did not cost money today but is worth recording precisely.** The trail's last raise used **live 311.25** (log: `STOP RAISED AAPL 308.48 -> 308.88 (live 311.25, peak 311.19)`), but the true session high five minutes later was **311.48**. **IMP-031 gave the BREAK-EVEN stage `max(live, peak_high_since_entry)`; the TRAIL stage still reads the 60s live point-sample only.** Worth at most **0.23 × 8 = $1.84** here (and $0.00 realised, since the stop was never hit). **Same defect class as IMP-031, one stage over.** Not shipped — see "Lessons" item 4.

**#263 UNH — LOSS −$15.36 (−0.42R), the day's only real loss, and it is an entry-quality failure.** Entered **10:16:34 @397.45**, into a name whose session high (**398.62**) was already behind it. MFE was **+0.15R at 11:11** — the trade was green on **32 of 296 minutes (11%)** and spent the rest underwater, bleeding to a 394.67 low **in the flatten minute itself**. Root cause is **not** stop placement (the 391.37 stop was never within 0.46R of being hit), **not** slippage, **not** a bug: it is that the MA crossover fired long on **the only name on the board sitting below both its 20MA and 50MA** (−4.38% / −4.71% per the 08-18 research log) on a **−0.68% risk-off tape with no catalyst**. That is a countertrend long in a downtrending name on a down day. Session range **0.82× 1R** — as with QQQ on 08-17 and SPY on 08-14, neither bracket leg was physically reachable.

**#264 WMT — SCRATCH −$2.54 (−0.07R), and it is the FOURTH near-miss on the +0.5R trigger.** The VWAP gate refused it **twice** (10:22:03 at +0.27%, 10:23:09 at +0.28% over session VWAP) and admitted it at **10:24:16 @115.41** once the extension fell under the 0.25% bar — the gate behaved exactly as designed. WMT had gapped to a 116.20 open / 116.28 high and was already −0.68% off that open at entry. It then rallied back to **116.28 at 11:09 — MFE +0.49R — and faded to a 115.20 close.**
- ★ **MFE +0.49R against a `BREAKEVEN_TRIGGER_R` / `TRAIL_TRIGGER_R` of 0.50R. It missed arming break-even by 0.01R** and consequently gave back **0.56R ($20.69)** from its peak to exit −0.07R. **The near-miss cluster is now n=4 and every member is just UNDER the trigger: CRM +0.45R, NVDA +0.43R, META +0.46R, WMT +0.49R.** That is a distributional fact worth naming — **the 0.5R trigger appears to be sited at the top edge of this book's MFE distribution, so it almost never arms.** ⚠️ **It is NOT a licence to lower the trigger: IMP-032 already swept exactly this parameter and found every row inside a $248.18 noise budget with non-monotone neighbours.** Recorded as evidence for the weekly, not acted on. See "Lessons" item 4.

### What worked / what didn't
- ★✅ **IMP-034 got its FIRST DECISIVE LIVE CASE, and it passed a pre-registered test.** The 08-17 entry recorded IMP-034 as "POSITIVE but WEAK — a single position and an easy 2.87s fill, not yet tested by a slow fill in production." **08-18 tested it.** Broker timeline: bracket stops cancelled **19:55:22.802Z**, limit legs **19:55:25.153–25.164Z** (2.36s — inside the 8s cancel budget), liquidations created **19:55:25.459–25.684Z** (0.30s after the shares released), submitted 19:55:27.11–27.29, **filled 19:55:31.097 / 33.870 / 34.263Z**. The fill wait began at ~19:55:25.7 and the last fill landed **8.6s later**. **The retired 8s shared budget would have expired at ~19:55:33.7 — before WMT's fill and 0.17s after UNH's — reproducing 08-14's spurious "EOD flatten incomplete" on a book that was flat.** It did not fire: `bot.log` shows the three FLATTEN lines and **no "incomplete"**, and DB `exit_time`s (15:55:35.2 / 37.0 / 39.8) are same-poll, not deferred. **Three-position book, one pass, ~9s end to end, verifiably flat. This is the strongest evidence any IMP has produced in two weeks.**
- ✅ **IMP-035 passes its pass-condition (a NULL result) for the second session.** All three fills scored 60.08–60.83 and every one sized at the 0.5% risk floor — identical to what the retired 4x ladder would have granted in the `[60,70)` band. `scripts.check_sizing_ladder` **ALL GREEN**, and it now reports `post-IMP-021 n=61, conf 60.00..64.17, tiers: 0.5%x61` with **0 post-veto entries carrying `breakout_score > 0`**. The alarm it exists to raise did not fire.
- ✅ **IMP-036 reproduces on the live tree.** `scripts.entry_discriminator` re-run today returns **NOT SUPPORTED at every threshold — ERA_ARTEFACT ×6, REFUTED ×2** for `atr-1r`, with the collateral warning naming TSLA (+$236.94 over 4 refused trades) and the era note that **97–104% of the refused cohort's P&L is the pre-gate week**. Full suite **297 passed**, matching the commit's claim.
- ★❌ **IMP-036's most valuable output is that it CLOSES a defect five daily reviews were carrying as the bot's #1 structural problem.** The "range-vs-1R mismatch" (SPY/QQQ trades whose session range is a fraction of their stop distance) was recorded as the top engine finding on 08-13, 08-14, 08-17 and twice in pre-market. **Run through the discriminator, the inverted low-range form comes back NOT SUPPORTED at every threshold.** Today supplies three more data points that say the same thing from the other side: UNH at 0.82× and WMT at 0.99× session-range/1R were the two **losers**, and AAPL at 1.21× was the winner — but AAPL's 1.21× still could not reach a 1.42R take-profit. **The mismatch is real as a description and worthless as a filter.** It should stop being listed as the top defect.
- ❌ **The entry signal still has no demonstrated edge, and the one winner is the cleanest possible illustration of why.** AAPL rose on an EU App Store settlement and two analyst actions — **information the strategy has no access to.** It bought the right name for a reason it cannot represent, sized identically to UNH, which it bought countertrend into a risk-off tape. **1W/2L with a 3.02 payoff on n=3 is a lucky draw, not a signal.** All-time: **250 closed, PF ~0.60, expectancy negative.**

**#267 AAPL — WIN +$49.26, and it is the cleanest trade in the bot's recorded history.** Bought 11 minutes after the open at 311.62, into a name that opened 310.145 and closed 316.90. It was green on **every single one of its 83 minutes**, its worst drawdown was **−0.04R ($0.20/share)**, and it ran to +1.55R before the 318.65 limit filled at 318.657143. The trail ratcheted **seven times** (306.96 → 312.47 → 312.80 → 313.12 → 313.80 → 314.21 → 314.67 → 314.99, 09:53 → 10:04) and then four more times (315.33 → 315.72 → 316.04) — but note that **none of it mattered**: the take-profit did the work and the trail never came within 2.6 points of being hit. Nothing to fix here; this is the strategy working as designed.
**#268 CRM — WIN +$49.20, and it is the trade that exposed today's defect.** Bought 09:46:53. Green on 57 of 58 minutes, MAE −0.05R, TP filled at 204.50. **But look at the geometry: the take-profit sat only 1.19R above the fill, not the configured 1.5R.** The plan was anchored to the **signal-bar close of 199.98** (stop 196.98 = the 1.5% floor, TP 204.48 = signal + 1.5×3.00); the market order filled at **200.40, 0.21% higher**. That single adverse tick did two things at once — it widened real risk from 3.00 to **3.42/share** and narrowed real reward from 4.50 to **4.08/share**, turning a planned **1.50:1 into a realised 1.19:1**. Sizing had already committed 12 shares against the 3.00 plan risk, so the trade carried **$41.04 at risk against a $36.00 budget (+14.0%)**. It won, so it cost nothing today — which is precisely why it had gone unnoticed. **This is IMP-037.**
**#269 AAPL — LOSS −$1.37 (−0.03R), a scratch, and correctly judged as noise.** Re-entered at 15:29:06 — **51 seconds before the 15:30 entry cutoff** — at 316.5614, after AAPL had already run 310.14 → ~316.6. It was **green on 0 of its 26 minutes**, MFE +0.02R, MAE −0.28R, and the 15:55 flatten took it out for −$1.37. The obvious reading is "a late entry has no time to reach a 1.48R target while the stop is fully live" — **I tested that and it is REFUTED** (see below). The honest root cause is **entry timing within the move**: it bought the top of a completed 2% run, not the start of one. One trade, −0.03R, no action warranted.

### What worked / what didn't
- ★✅ **The exit layer had its best day of the incubation, and it was the TAKE-PROFIT that delivered it.** Two limit fills, zero stops, and both winners banked inside 83 minutes. This is worth naming because the last six sessions all closed 100% on the clock with decorative brackets; today the brackets were the exit. All-time the TP bucket remains the best the bot owns: **27 trades, 100% win, +$2,132.21, avg +$78.97.**
- ★❌ **The sizing defect found today is real, mechanical, and has been silently active since day one — and it was already documented.** `plan_position` sizes `floor(budget / stop_distance)` off the **signal-bar close**, but the order is a MARKET buy filling **live**; when price runs up inside the accepted slippage band the stop does not follow, so real risk/share is `stop_distance + move`. **IMP-008 wrote this down verbatim on 2026-06-30** — *"the stop now sits that much further from the real fill, silently inflating per-share risk above the plan"* — and then shipped only the guard that **skips** gaps beyond 1.0%, leaving the entire accepted 0–1.0% band sizing off a risk it does not take. Measured over all 253 closed trades: **106 (41.9%) risked more than their budget**, mean overshoot **+8.6%**, worst **SE 2026-07-02 at $144.54 against an $83.81 budget (+72.5%)**. Post-gate: **34 of 64 (53.1%)**. The ceiling is structural — `MAX_ENTRY_SLIPPAGE_PCT` 1.0% over a `MIN_STOP_PCT` 1.5% stop admits **+67%** more risk than budgeted, so a trade sized at the 0.5% floor rung can quietly risk ~0.83% of equity. **Fixed as IMP-037.**
- ✅ **IMP-035 passes its NULL pass-condition for the third session.** All three fills scored 60.45–62.11 and every one sized at the 0.5% floor. `scripts.check_sizing_ladder` **ALL GREEN**: `post-IMP-021 n=64, conf 60.00..64.17, tiers: 0.5%x64`, **0 post-veto entries with `breakout_score > 0`**. The alarm did not fire.
- ✅ **IMP-036 earned its keep for the second consecutive review, and again by saying NO.** Today's obvious candidate — *"the 15:29 entry had only 26 minutes before the flatten; refuse entries too close to the cutoff"* — has a believable mechanism and a fresh losing trade behind it. Run through `bot/discriminator.py` it comes back **NOT SUPPORTED at every threshold from 240 to 345 minutes after the open**, and the reason is that it is **backwards**: late entries are the bot's **best** cohort — at ≥315 min (14:45 ET) they are **n=11, +$76.25, PF 4.60, era-controlled identical** (all 11 are post-pre-gate), against a book that loses. Refusing them has **negative** edge at every threshold (−$3.46 to −$20.01/trade) with 122–128% collateral. **A filter I would have shipped on intuition would have removed a profitable cohort.**
- ✅ **The discriminator ALSO refuted the first form of today's real finding, which is why IMP-037 took the shape it did.** Testing *"refuse entries whose fill is more than X% above the signal close"* gives a **positive edge in all three cohorts at every threshold** (+$2.28 to +$26.06/trade) and — unusually — **passes the era check outright** (pre-gate share of the refused cohort's P&L is **0%**; this is a wholly modern effect, unlike the ATR/1R trap). It is nonetheless **REFUTED on collateral**: at +0.10–0.20% it discards **51–65%** worth of net-positive symbols (**GOOGL +$87.03, META +$44.67, BAC +$36.44, AAPL +$32.32**) against a 25% cap. **That refutation directly shaped the fix**: the problem is not that these trades should not be taken, it is that they are sized wrong. IMP-037 **refuses nothing** and therefore inherits none of the collateral objection.
- ⚠️ **The VWAP gate had its most active session on record (92 refusals across 11 symbols) and its 08-19 scorecard is genuinely MIXED — recorded, deliberately not acted on.** It refused **TSLA ×22** (at +1.2…+1.4% over VWAP; TSLA closed **+3.62%**), **NFLX ×12** (**+2.58%**), **AMZN ×10** (**+1.93%**) and **CRM ×10** in the afternoon (CRM ran on to 207.94, another ~+1.7% past the TP) — but it also refused **GOOG ×21** (closed **+0.54%**, a 1.59% chop) and **NVDA ×7** at +0.26…+0.35% (closed **−1.88%**). The mechanism is real and worth naming: **session VWAP is a lagging average, so on a trending day every name is extended above it all day and the gate refuses the whole tape** — a mean-reversion filter sitting on top of a trend-following signal. **But one day is not evidence, and the cross-day data says no:** over the 11 fully-logged sessions in the current log window, refusal count does not predict P&L at all (100 refusals → −$92.27 on 08-06; 92 → **+$97.09** today; **0 → −$71.51** on 08-14). IMP-022's 07-31 green-tape replay also showed the gate dodging stop-outs, not winners. **Keep the flat +0.25% cut. Do not make it tape-conditional on the strength of one session.** Registered trigger for the weekly below.
- ⚠️ **A ~82-second unprotected window on AAPL #267, in a winning trade, cause not determined.** Broker record: the trail's last stop leg (`40bb9828`, 316.04) was created 15:02:24.116Z and went **`status=canceled` at 15:02:57.421Z with `replaced_by` null** — i.e. it was *cancelled*, not *replaced* — and the take-profit did not fill until **15:04:19.190Z**. `execution.replace_stop_order`'s docstring asserts *"a failed replace leaves the old stop working, so the position is never unprotected"*; **this timeline is inconsistent with that assertion.** There was **no `STOP RAISE FAILED` line, no error, and no exception** in the log, and the bot's own `flatten_all` is the only code path that cancels, which does not run until 19:55Z. The most likely explanation is Alpaca's **paper OCO releasing the stop leg once the limit became marketable** — AAPL was within **$0.04** of the 318.65 limit at that moment — which would make it broker mechanics, not a bot defect. Note CRM's stop cancelled **2 ms after** its TP filled, the normal OCO ordering, so the behaviour is not consistent even within one session. **Not actionable on n=1, and it occurred at the most profitable point of a winning trade rather than at a risk moment.** Escalated to the weekly with a concrete test below.
- ❌ **The entry signal still has no demonstrated edge, and one very good day does not touch that.** All-time after today: **253 closed, −$2,199.19, PF 0.62, expectancy −$8.69/trade, win rate 38.7%.** Post-gate: **64 trades, −$174.56, PF 0.73.** Today's +$97.09 came from two MA-crossover longs that happened to be bought at 09:41 and 09:46 into a +2.2% and a +5.1% name — **AAPL on an analyst AI note and CRM on a bounce, neither of which the strategy can represent.** A 66.7% win rate and a PF of 71.9 on **n=3** is a lucky draw. **The weekly's verdict stands verbatim and I am not softening it on a green day.**
- ❌ **The breakout leg contributed 0.0000 for the sixth consecutive session.** Dormant since 07-24. IMP-035's reason for leaving it in the scorer (removing it risks a confidence-scale shift that re-arms the sizing tier IMP-035 just disarmed) still holds. **Keep watching, do not tidy.**
- ✅ **Process: the 08-18 record/deploy gap is fully closed and did not recur.** IMP-036 is on the remote, logged, and reproduces on the live tree; this run committed, pushed, deployed and logged in sequence with verification at each step. **Fourth-occurrence watch remains open** — the escalation in IMP-036's entry stands.

### Lessons & improvement candidates
1. **★ SHIPPED — IMP-037: size on the LIVE entry risk, never the planned risk.** The single highest-impact change today's data supports, and it completes a hole the repo has carried, documented and unfixed, since **IMP-008 on 2026-06-30**. `sizing.resize_for_live_risk()` re-derives the share count against `live_price − stop_price` and is **clamped to `min(planned, live-risk)`**, so a favourable fill can never buy extra size — it is **strictly risk-reducing**. Stop, take-profit, `stop_distance` and the risk fraction are untouched, so **1R and every downstream R-multiple stay comparable across the whole book**. **322 tests pass (+25 new).** ★ **Be clear about the direction of the money: on today's tape this change would have COST $8.00** (CRM 12 → 10 shares, +$49.20 → +$41.00; the day +$97.09 → +$89.09). It is net-positive era-controlled (**+$51.66**) and post-gate (**+$28.11**) only as a *side effect* of the over-risked cohort being net-negative. **It ships as a risk-accounting correction — the bot must risk what it says it risks — not as an edge claim.**
2. **The capital question is unchanged and remains the only one that matters.** Equity **$7,504.01 (−24.96%)**, back over the line by **$4.01** after eight sessions below it, on a 253-trade book with PF 0.62. **Three green days totalling +$111.67 do not constitute a turnaround** and I decline to let this entry read as one. The **−25% escalation is still an OPEN, UNANSWERED human decision point.**
3. **REFUTED and recorded so it is never re-proposed: "no entries in the last N minutes before the cutoff."** Era-controlled at seven thresholds (240–345 min after the open) the edge is **negative everywhere**; late entries are the bot's best cohort (≥14:45 ET: n=11, +$76.25, PF 4.60). Belongs in `todo.md` "Refuted / closed candidates".
4. **REFUTED and recorded: "refuse entries filled more than X% above the signal close."** Positive edge in all three cohorts and a clean era check, **killed on collateral** (51–65% of net-positive symbols; GOOGL/META/BAC/AAPL). Superseded in the right way by IMP-037, which fixes the sizing rather than discarding the trades.
5. **Two items registered for the WEEKLY, with explicit triggers, deliberately not acted on today:** (i) **The VWAP gate on trend days** — the mechanism (session VWAP lags, so a trending tape is uniformly "extended") is real, and today it refused three names that ran +1.9…+3.6%. **Trigger: if a second high-refusal session shows the same one-sided miss pattern, run the refused cohort through `bot/discriminator.py` with a counterfactual replay before touching `VWAP_MAX_DIST_PCT`.** Today's cross-day check found no relationship, so nothing moves yet. (ii) **The 82-second unprotected window** — **concrete test: pull every TAKE_PROFIT exit's bracket timeline from Alpaca and measure the gap between the stop leg's `canceled_at` and the limit leg's `filled_at`.** If the gap is consistently positive across the 27 TP exits it is broker mechanics and the `replace_stop_order` docstring needs correcting; if it is sporadic it is a real hole in the ratchet.

- ★❌ **REFUTED — the concurrency-pacing idea, carried since 2026-08-11 as "the strongest un-refuted entry-side idea", is dead.** Run through `bot/discriminator.py` (encoding: value 1.0 for a trade that was the **3rd concurrent** position **and** entered within X minutes of the open, 0.0 otherwise; threshold 1.0), refusing that cohort is **NOT SUPPORTED at any window**: X=10 **ERA_ARTEFACT**, X=15 **INSUFFICIENT_DATA**, X=20/30/45/60 **REFUTED**. ### Lessons & improvement candidates
1. **★ SHIPPED — IMP-038: `manage_stops` must tell a closed position apart from an unprotected one.** The `stop_leg is None → continue` branch now asks the broker whether the position is actually open; if it is, the bot logs `UNPROTECTED <SYM>`, raises **one** alert per position per day, and — **only if the tape is already at or below the trade's ORIGINAL plan stop** — cancels the sibling limit leg and closes the position (`SOFT STOP`). **It can only ever remove exposure**: it never invents a tighter stop, never touches a protected position, fails quiet on an unreadable price, and degrades to the old behaviour on a broker-lookup failure. **349 tests pass (+27), self-contained (326 on a clean HEAD + these three files), all four check scripts green.** On the recorded book it changes **no trade's P&L by one cent** — all ten windows sat above their stop. **No edge is claimed.**
2. **The capital question is unchanged, and today it got worse in the way that matters.** Equity **$7,457.40 (−25.43%)**, back below the line after two sessions above it; 256 trades; PF 0.62. Three green days did not constitute a turnaround, as this entry declined to claim on 08-19, and one red day does not constitute a collapse. **The −25% escalation is an open human decision point in its ninth session: fund it, retire it, or halve the size.** No IMP I can ship changes that sentence, and IMP-038 explicitly does not pretend to.
3. **REFUTED and recorded so it is never re-proposed: concurrency pacing ("do not spend all three slots in ten minutes").** Numbers and encoding above; belongs in `todo.md` "Refuted / closed candidates" as the fourteenth failure. **Do not reopen on the strength of another day where the book froze early and the tape reversed** — that is what 08-10, 08-11 and today all look like, and era-controlled it is the profitable cohort.
4. **Registered for the WEEKLY, deliberately not acted on: the +0.5R break-even trigger's near-miss cluster is now n=5** (CRM 0.45, NVDA 0.43, META 0.46, WMT 0.49, **TSM 0.43** today). Five trades peaking between 0.43R and 0.49R and none arming is starting to look less like coincidence than the 08-19 entry allowed — **but IMP-032 swept exactly this parameter across a $248.18 noise budget with non-monotone neighbours, and the standing weekly instruction is that no exit-geometry change is resolvable in this sample.** **Trigger for the weekly: at n≥8, re-run the sweep on the grown sample with a held-out split.** Nothing moves before then.
5. **The `EOD_FLATTEN` labelling limitation of IMP-038 is a known, accepted debt** — a `SOFT STOP` liquidation will be booked by the 15:55 flatten at the correct real fill but with the wrong reason. Fires 0 times in 256 trades; relabel by hand if it ever fires, and only then consider a proper booking path.
6. **Not shipped, recorded: re-arming a real broker stop (cancel the marketable limit, resubmit an OCO sell) during a naked window.** Correct in principle and it is what a human would do; rejected because a partial failure leaves the position with **no exit orders at all**. Needs a dry-run harness against the paper account before it can be considered.

### What worked / what didn't
- ★★❌ **IMP-038 fired for the first time — ON THE WRONG THING — and the false positive exposes a path to a naked short. This is IMP-039.** At **12:04:22 ET** the bot logged `UNPROTECTED UNH: position open with no working bracket stop leg (live 390.61, plan stop 384.09)` and raised a Telegram alert. UNH #275 was **not** unprotected: its stop leg had been trailed to 390.55 and was in the act of **selling** the position. Broker timestamps read back through the `alpaca` MCP settle it exactly — limit leg `52f81840` canceled **16:04:19.610486Z**, stop leg `c3e90c63` submitted **16:04:19.672162Z**, **filled 16:04:22.502483Z @ 390.523333**. The 12:04:22 poll landed **inside that second**: `resolve_stop_leg` returned None (FILLED is not a PATCHable status) while `GET /v2/positions` still listed UNH, so the engine inferred "open, no stop". **The alarm is the cheap half.** The expensive half: `naked_position_action` enforces at/below the trade's **ORIGINAL** plan stop, and **every ordinary stop-out satisfies that by construction** — a stop fills at or through its own price. **111 of 261 closed trades exited on STOP.** **MU #273 missed the identical window by 53 seconds this morning** (stop filled 14:19:01.365898Z @ 961.31 against a 961.59 plan stop; next poll 14:19:54Z, position already gone). Had that poll landed where UNH's did, IMP-038 would have cancelled the sibling leg and sent `DELETE /v2/positions/MU` against a position the broker had already flattened — **a second sell on a flat book, i.e. a short**, which is the exact failure `_handle_naked_position`'s own docstring claims to rule out. **Shipped tonight; see Lessons #1.**
- ★❌ **MEASUREMENT DEFECT — the VWAP gate ledger overstates itself by ~2x, and it has been doing so all along.** 51 `ENTRY SKIPPED` lines were logged today, but **26 of them (25 GOOG + 1 UNH) were for symbols the bot ALREADY HELD.** `consider_entries` evaluates the VWAP gate at `engine.py:302`, **before** the underlying-held guard at `engine.py:313` — so a held name is re-scored, re-refused and re-logged on every poll even though it was never an eligible candidate. **Real refusals today: 25 (AMD 13, MSFT 5, TSLA 3, META 3, CRM 1).** No trading consequence — the entry is skipped either way, and there is **no pyramiding hole** (`equivalent_symbols` includes the symbol itself, so a held name is unconditionally skipped) — but **the gate ledger is a primary input to these reviews and to the weekly**, and the 08-19 entry's "the VWAP gate refused 92 times" is now suspect for the same reason. **Registered as the leading IMP-040 candidate.**
- ✅ **The book turned over and the 08-20 slot-freeze did not repeat.** Five entries across 5h20m, one slot recycled after MU's stop. The gate refused 25 real candidates (AMD 13 times at +0.59…+0.73%, TSLA 3 times at +1.57…+1.81%) — **TSLA in particular was refused at nearly 2% above VWAP, which is precisely the stretched-fill cohort IMP-022 was built to refuse.** This is the gate working as designed, and it is the reason the day was −0.84R instead of worse.
- ✅ **IMP-013's break-even ratchet earned its keep on UNH** (+$3.98 booked vs +$1.56 for holding to the flatten) and **armed correctly on GOOG**. Two of five trades reached the +0.5R trigger — the best arming rate in a fortnight.
- ⚠️ **The +0.5R near-miss cluster grows to n=6: MU +0.44R** joins CRM 0.45, NVDA 0.43, META 0.46, WMT 0.49, TSM 0.43. **The standing weekly trigger is n≥8 for a re-run of the IMP-032 sweep with a held-out split.** Nothing moves before then; recorded only.
- ❌ **The entry signal still has no demonstrated edge, and nothing today changes that.** 261 trades, PF 0.613, expectancy −$8.71; post-gate 72 trades, PF 0.662, −$3.45/trade. Eighth straight session with `breakout_score` 0.0000 on every signal — **the bot has not taken a breakout trade in a month and is a pure MA-crossover system wearing a breakout system's name.** Two of today's five trades (META, SPY) were green on 0% and 37% of their minutes; the bot cannot tell those apart at entry, and fifteen refuted discriminators say it will not learn to. **The weekly's verdict stands verbatim.**
- ⚠️ **Sonar returned a full day of stale index data and would have been believed without the bar cross-check.** The standing "verify against own bars" rule is what caught it. **Restate it as mandatory, not advisory.**

### Lessons & improvement candidates
1. **★ SHIPPED — IMP-039: a stop leg that FILLED is not a missing stop leg.** New `exits.stop_leg_filled()` walks the same replace chain `resolve_stop_leg` does and reports whether the leg reached **FILLED / PARTIALLY_FILLED**; `manage_stops` now skips the naked-position branch entirely on that state, **before** the `/v2/positions` lookup. **CANCELED — which is what Alpaca's OCO does to the stop leg on the take-profit path, and therefore what all ten of IMP-038's measured naked windows actually were — still alerts and still enforces, unchanged.** `resolve_stop_leg` was refactored onto a shared walker with its contract pinned by parametrized non-regression tests. **386 tests pass (+37)**, in a new `tests/test_naked_stop_race.py` built on today's real broker timestamps: the UNH #275 regression (silent), its CANCELED control (still alerts), the **MU #273 counterfactual** (a filled stop at/below the plan stop is **not** liquidated a second time) and its CANCELED control (still liquidated), partially-filled, the no-extra-API-call assertion, and an invariant test asserting the guard can only ever **narrow** what gets sold. All four `check_*` scripts green, `scripts.smoke_test` ALL GREEN. **On the recorded book it changes no trade's P&L by one cent; it removes one false alarm and closes one path to an unintended short.**
2. **★ REGISTERED FOR IMP-040 — move the VWAP gate below the underlying-held guard in `consider_entries`.** A pure ordering change (`engine.py:302` after `engine.py:313`) with **zero trading effect** and one real benefit: the gate ledger stops double-counting held names, which makes every future "the gate refused N times" number mean what it says. Cheap, testable, and it repairs a measurement this review depends on. **Do not bundle it with anything.**
3. **The capital question is unchanged and now in its eleventh session.** Equity **$7,429.61 (−25.70%)**, 261 trades, PF 0.613. **The −25% escalation is an open human decision point: fund it, retire it, or halve the size.** No IMP I can ship changes that sentence, and IMP-039 explicitly does not pretend to — it is a safety fix, not an edge.
4. **NOT shipped, registered for the WEEKLY: the stop floor is anchored to the wrong things, and MU is the proof.** `MIN_STOP_PCT` 1.5% produced a **0.29-ATR** stop on MU (ATR 6.03%) and a **3.1-session-range** stop on SPY (range 0.48%) on the same day — too tight on the volatile name, absurdly wide on the quiet one. A flat percentage floor cannot serve both. **This is exit geometry, it is owned by the weekly, IMP-032 already swept it once across a $248 noise budget, and one session cannot resolve it.** ⚠️ It has now been escalated as an unowned finding on 08-20, 08-21 and today — **the weekly must either take it or refuse it in writing.**
5. **Also registered, not acted on: the plan stop is anchored to the SIGNAL price, not the fill.** MU's 961.59 was 1.500% below the 976.23 signal but **1.748% below the 978.70 fill.** IMP-037 fixed the *sizing* consequence; the *geometry* consequence (1R means something different from what the plan said, and the break-even/trail triggers are computed off it) is untouched. **Weekly, alongside #4 — they are the same conversation.**
6. **Do not re-propose "no late entries".** META's 15:01 entry was the day's third-worst trade and the temptation is obvious. **That is a one-trade observation of the exact shape that has produced fifteen refuted discriminators**, and IMP-036's era-controlled harness exists precisely to stop it. If it is ever proposed it must go through `bot/discriminator.py` first, on the whole book, era-controlled.

Not a loss and not a signal failure; a *capture* failure — the 0.5R trail sat far enough below price that a 4h07m hold converted to a fifth of its peak.
- **CRM #281 — the break-even stop working exactly as designed.** Peaked **+0.54R** (213.09), armed break-even at 10:01:49 (stop → 211.15 vs a 211.149 fill), stopped 12 minutes later for **−$0.39**. This is a *good* outcome mechanically. It is also the day's clearest evidence of the structural point below: it took a +0.54R excursion to arm anything at all. **IMP-037 fired here for the first time ever** — `SIZE REDUCED CRM: 11 → 10 shares (real risk/share 3.6100 vs planned 3.1600)` — the fill came in 0.21% above the signal, and the live-risk resize correctly refused to buy more risk than budgeted. After five inert sessions the weekly called it "correct by construction, unproven in the wild"; it is now proven.

It then drifted for 5h41m and was flattened at −0.31R for **−$10.45**. MAE −0.39R — it never came close to its 1R stop either. A trade that showed profit, was never protected, and was ultimately decided by the 15:55 clock.

**Root-cause grouping.** Zero losses today were caused by signal quality, stop distance, slippage, bad bars, re-entry, or a code bug. All four stops sat at 1.52–1.72% of entry (the `MIN_STOP_PCT` 1.5% floor), none was hit. **The single recurring root cause is exit capture: the book generated $2,140 of aggregate open profit across four trades and banked $13.57 of it.** Three of four trades peaked between +0.26R and +0.80R — i.e. *entirely inside the band where the 0.5R ratchet does nothing useful* — and two of four were decided by the clock rather than by any trading decision.

### Market context
Perplexity `sonar` errored (`Connection reset by peer`), so this is from the tape and the bot's own SIP bars rather than a news read. 2026-08-24 was the **light day of a heavy week**: no watchlist name reported, the only calendar items were the Chicago Fed National Activity Index pre-open and a 3-month bill auction. **Worked**

### What worked / what didn't
**Worked**
- **Reconciliation is clean and the broker-vs-DB drift did not recur.** Sixteen orders, four fills, two stop fills, two flattens — all matching, no drift, flat overnight.

- **IMP-039 stayed clean on its second full session**, including two genuine stop-leg fills (TSM, META) — exactly the state that produced 08-21's false alarm. No false `UNPROTECTED`.
- **The reconstruction in `scripts/ratchet_audit.py` reproduces the live bot exactly** — 0/4 fidelity gaps on 08-25, and it re-derives both live `STOP RAISED` prices (416.02, 565.32) to the cent.

**Didn't**
- **IMP-040's first live session posted its own pre-registered FAIL signature, on a sample far too small to act on.** IMP-040 wrote: *"Fail = winners visibly cut short with no reduction in the give-back cohort."* Today: the day's one real winner was visibly cut short (META, −$15.95 foregone), **and** the give-back cohort was not reduced (AMZN and SPY both still went to the clock; EOD_FLATTEN 50% vs a post-gate 58.8% is nothing at n=4). **This is one session and four trades. Harmless today — market closed, book flat, `NRestarts=0`, clean startup. **Checked and it is safe by construction, not by luck**: the timer is `OnCalendar 06:00` *local* (WIB) + 60m jitter = a **23:00–00:00 UTC window**, which sits 2–3 hours after every US close in both EDT and EST. Recorded as a known benign event so a future run does not re-investigate it as an anomaly.

### Lessons & improvement candidates
1. **★★ SHIPPED as IMP-041 — the exit mix, the instrument every review reads, is conflated, and tonight proved it hides the actual diagnosis.** `trades.exit_reason` has ONE string for a stop-out. Decomposed by fill price against the plan stop, the post-gate book's `STOP n=30, net −$471.16` is really **`PLAN_STOP` n=14 (17.5%), net −$524.56, avg −$37.47** against **`RATCHET_STOP` n=16 (20.0%), net +$53.40, avg +$3.34**. It reconciles exactly to the book (−524.56 + 53.40 + 154.18 + 68.17 = −$248.81). **The whole post-gate loss is 14 trades out of 80.** Every other bucket, including the clock-decided one, is net positive. Full detail in `memory/improvement-log.md`.
2. **★★ That decomposition contradicts the premise IMP-040 was built on, and it should be the next real conversation — after IMP-040's window closes.** IMP-040 targeted the clock-decided population. **The clock-decided population makes money (`EOD_FLATTEN` n=47, +$68.17).** The money is lost in 14 full-1R stop-outs at −$37.47 each. **Do not act on this yet** — it is a book-level observation, IMP-040 is mid-experiment, and the fix (entry quality or stop anchor) touches the two areas most likely to destroy attribution. Re-measure when IMP-040 reports.
3. **★ The ratchet's realised lifetime P&L is now measurable and it is roughly a wash: −$11.67 over 16 ratchet-stop exits** (helped 10 for +$71.29, hurt 6 for −$82.95), of which **−$17.46 is 08-25 alone**. Pre-IMP-040 it was **+$5.79**. Two more weeks of this number is exactly what IMP-040's verdict needs.
4. **★ A structural note on the ratchet's SHAPE, logged now for the IMP-040 verdict and deliberately not acted on.** `TRAIL_DISTANCE_R == TRAIL_TRIGGER_R` means the trail is **born at break-even**: at the instant it arms, the stop equals the entry price, so the trade has zero give-back room. At a 0.25R trigger it therefore arms *inside the instrument's own noise* — META survived a **−0.45R** drawdown and was then killed by a **−0.29R** one, purely because the ratchet had armed in between. **If the 2-week verdict is FAIL, decoupling `TRAIL_DISTANCE_R` from `TRAIL_TRIGGER_R` is a better-motivated response than IMP-040's pre-registered "widen back to 0.35R"**, because it fixes the shape rather than sliding a shape that has none. Do not ship it on n=2.
5. **(Refused) Any exit-geometry change tonight.** 08-25 was IMP-040's FIRST live session. Re-tuning the ratchet on four trades would destroy the only experiment currently running, over-fit to one day, and violate IMP-040's own pre-registration. Pinned by `test_imp040_geometry_is_untouched_by_this_run`.
6. **(Refused) A late-entry filter — measured tonight and REFUTED.** SPY #289's 54-minute hold looked like an obvious cut, so it was tested: post-gate trades with **≤60 minutes to the flatten are n=8, net −$3.57, avg −$0.45** — the *least* damaging bucket on the board. The damage is in the **09:30–10:00 entries: 60 of 80 trades and −$244.59 of the −$248.81**. Filtering the late trades would remove the cheapest trades and leave the expensive ones. **Recorded so no future run re-proposes it.**
7. **(Refused) Anything on the entry layer.** Fifteen discriminators are refuted; the standing rule is that new entry filters go through `bot/discriminator.py` era-controlled or not at all. Today gives no entry-layer evidence — 3 of 4 entries were directionally right.
8. **(Refused) Reacting to the apt-triggered restart.** Investigated, benign by construction (23:00–00:00 UTC window, ≥2h after every close), no code change warranted.

- **MSFT #292 — the day's only real trade, and the first live session where IMP-040 clearly beat what it replaced.** Signal 493.24, filled 0.137% *below* it at 492.566. Ratchet chain, all four raises verified against the broker: **485.84 → 492.57** (break-even, armed on the *peak* 494.25 = +0.2497R, clearing the 0.25R trigger by two hundredths of an R) **→ 493.07 → 493.70 → 495.22** (each a live-price − 0.25R trail). Stopped at 495.16 for **+$12.97 / +0.386R** after **14m41s**. MFE was **+0.680R** (497.14 at 09:50) and MSFT then spent the entire remaining session between +0.15R and +0.68R before closing the 15:55 bar at 496.91. **Held to the flatten it makes +$21.72, so the ratchet cost −$8.75.** But that is the wrong counterfactual for judging IMP-040: replayed under the **pre-IMP-040 0.5R/0.5R** geometry the same trade exits at ~493.24 for **+$3.36**, so IMP-040 is **+$9.61 ahead of its own predecessor on this trade**. Note also the MAE: **+0.032R** — MSFT never traded below the fill. Not a signal failure in any sense.
- **TSM #293 — the archetype, for the third consecutive session.** Signal 418.03, filled 418.60 (+0.136% adverse; live-risk was checked and 5 shares was correct under both the planned and live risk-per-share, so IMP-037 rightly stayed silent). **MFE +0.101R, reached inside the first minutes and never again.** It sank to **−0.613R** (414.41), survived its 1R stop, and drifted **6h18m49s** to the clock for **−$8.55**. Nothing ever armed — its peak never came within a sixth of the 0.25R trigger. Same shape as AAPL #283 (08-24) and AMZN #286 (08-25). **This cohort is not reachable by ratchet geometry of any width, and its designated lever — the never-green time-stop — was REFUTED by IMP-032 across a 42-cell grid.** See "Lessons" item 3.
- **QQQ #294 and SPY #295 — two trades that could not resolve, entered five minutes apart.** QQQ 14:51 (1h04m to the flatten), SPY 14:56 (58m). QQQ's *entire* excursion over the hold was **0.20R**; SPY's was **0.12R**. Both are the SPY #289 (08-25) trade repeated: a **1.5%-floor stop on instruments whose ATRs are 1.18% and 0.63%**, so SPY's stop sat **2.4× its own average daily range** away and neither bracket leg could physically fire. Combined cost **−$3.21** — cheap, and that cheapness is the point (see item 4).

**Root-cause grouping.** Zero losses were caused by signal quality, regime, slippage, bad bars, re-entry, or a code bug. **Zero PLAN_STOP exits — no trade came within 40% of its 1R stop.** Every stop again sat at the `MIN_STOP_PCT` floor; `ATR_STOP_MULT = 3.0` bound on none of the four. The day splits into exactly two causes: **(1) one never-armed drifter (TSM, MFE +0.10R) that went to the clock for −$8.55**, and **(2) two structurally unresolvable index-ETF trades (−$3.21 combined)**. Against those, the ratchet banked the one trade that trended. **On a tape that closed flat, the book was +$1.21.**

### Market context
Perplexity `sonar` answered and its index-level read was usable and matched the tape. **2026-08-26 was a CHOPPY, drifting, mildly risk-off session — S&P 500 −0.02% (7,675.70), Nasdaq Composite −0.08% (26,130.20)** — an early bid on an in-line **core PCE (0.2% m/m / 3.3% y/y, exactly consensus)** that faded through the afternoon, with a **slightly warm headline PCE (3.7% y/y)** and positioning ahead of NVDA doing the rest. **Sonar was blind at the name level for MSFT, TSM and CRM** — the standing "regime lead only, never names" rule holds for a third run. **This matters for attribution and it cuts the opposite way from the last two sessions:** 08-24 and 08-25 were benign trending tapes on which the bot converted almost nothing. **08-26 was the no-trend day, and the bot came out slightly ahead** — because the one name that did trend (MSFT, which closed +0.7% against a flat index) was the one it banked, and the three that chopped cost $11.76 between them. That is the correct behaviour for a chop day, and it is worth recording that it happened *after* IMP-040 tightened the ratchet, not before.
**After the close: NVDA beat hard** — Q2 FY27 revenue **$96.22B vs ~$92.2B consensus** (guide was $91.0B ±2%) and non-GAAP EPS **$2.22 vs $2.09**. **CRM's print was not recoverable from sonar** and is handed to the pre-market run.

### What worked / what didn't
**Worked**
- **The ratchet banked a winner on a flat tape, and the honest counterfactual favours IMP-040.** MSFT +$12.97 vs ~+$3.36 under the old 0.5R geometry. Book-level over the 8-trade IMP-040 era (`scripts/exit_geometry --since 2026-08-25`): live 0.25R/0.25R sim **−$7.30** vs the pre-IMP-040 0.5R/0.5R sim **−$17.19** — **IMP-040 is +$9.89 ahead of what it replaced**, even while being −$26.51 against holding.
- **Reconciliation is clean and the broker-vs-DB one-way gap did NOT recur** (see Stats).
- **Risk discipline held.** 0.42–0.50% risk/trade, no halt approached, no naked-position alert, **zero `ERROR`/`Traceback`/`UNPROTECTED`/`SOFT STOP`/`FLATTEN REJECTED` lines all session**, `NRestarts=0`, clean 11:49:28 UTC start (the pre-market's watchlist restart).
- **IMP-039 stayed clean on its fourth full session**, including a genuine stop-leg fill (MSFT) — exactly the state that produced 08-21's false alarm.

**Didn't**
- **IMP-040's pre-registered FAIL signature is now 2-for-2, and the sample is still 8 trades.** Its text: *"Fail = winners visibly cut short with no reduction in the give-back cohort."* Both legs fired again — MSFT was visibly cut short (−$8.75 vs holding), and the **EOD_FLATTEN share went UP, 59.2% → 62.5%** across the IMP-040 boundary. Ratchet ledger for the era: **3 armed trades, 3 hurt, 0 helped, −$26.51** (pre-IMP-040 the lifetime ledger was +$5.79). **Recorded, not acted on** — see "Lessons" item 1 for why, including the arithmetic that cuts the other way.
- **The ATR stop term is inert for the third consecutive session.** Four trades, four stops at the 1.5% floor. `ATR_STOP_MULT = 3.0` has not set a stop this week.
- **The VWAP gate's own telemetry was measurably wrong, and today's log proved it.** 9 of the session's 31 `ENTRY SKIPPED` lines (29%) were **TSM — while TSM was in the book from 09:36 to the 15:55 flatten**. Fixed tonight as IMP-042; see below.
- **The strategy verdict is unchanged and I restate it plainly: this is not a breakout system and it has no demonstrated edge.** 24 sessions without a single breakout-scored fill; a confidence scorer that varied 3.08 points; all-time **273 closed trades, −$2,272.23**, `scripts.report` verdict **NEEDS WORK — expectancy not positive, false-breakout rate 52.7%**. Today's +$1.21 is a good draw from a negative distribution, and one green day after a chop session changes nothing about it. **The retire-or-rebuild decision escalated by the 08-22 weekly is still owed by a human. No exit-geometry or telemetry change substitutes for it.**

### Lessons & improvement candidates
1. **★★ (REFUSED, and this is the run's main judgement call) Do not touch IMP-040 tonight, despite its FAIL signature firing twice.** Three independent reasons, in order of weight. **(a) The sample is 8 trades and 3 armed trades**, against a pre-registration this log is bound by (*"judge on the exit mix over ~2 weeks"*); day 3 of ~10. **(b) The counterfactual that matters says IMP-040 is WINNING.** "−$26.51 against holding" is the wrong yardstick — the decision on the table was never "ratchet vs no ratchet", it was "0.25R vs the 0.5R it replaced", and on the same 8 trades that comparison is **−$7.30 vs −$17.19, i.e. +$9.89 for IMP-040**, with MSFT alone worth +$9.61 of it. **(c) The what-if grid's only noise-clearing rows are degenerate.** Every `trail@1R-*` row scores −$0.94 and "clears noise" — but a 1R trigger against an era whose *maximum* MFE was +0.68R **never arms at all**, so those rows are the null hypothesis wearing a geometry, exactly the "a rule whose optimum is *stop trading* is not a rule" trap this log already named once. **Let it run to its window.**
2. **★★ SHIPPED as IMP-042 — the VWAP gate was being asked about candidates that could not be bought, and the answer was being recorded as if it could.** Eligibility (held-skip / daily cap / cooldown) was evaluated *after* the quality gate, so an ineligible symbol that happened to be stretched was logged and counted as a VWAP refusal — and IMP-033's refused-candidate replay in `scripts/gate_monitor.py` then priced a counterfactual entry the engine could never have taken. Today: **TSM, 9 lines, 29% of the session's refusals, while held**, with the 13:57:39 line becoming the day's `_first_blocked` TSM candidate. Over the 8 sessions of retained logs: **4 of 45 first-blocked candidates (8.9%)** and **37 of 389 raw refusal lines (9.5%)**. Outcome-identical by construction, so IMP-040's window is untouched. Full detail in `memory/improvement-log.md`.
3. **(REFUSED — already refuted, recorded so TSM does not revive it) The never-green / stall time-stop.** TSM #293 is a textbook case (MFE +0.101R, 6h19m, −$8.55) and it is the third in three sessions. It is nonetheless **dead**: IMP-032 tested a 42-cell grid (X ∈ 0.15–0.5R, T ∈ 15–120min) and **every non-degenerate cell was negative**; the only positive cells cut ~45 of 51 trades and "work" by removing exposure. IMP-041's decomposition independently agrees — **`EOD_FLATTEN` is a net-POSITIVE bucket (n=50, +$56.41)**, so cutting it early is measurably wrong at book level. TSM is one bad draw from a bucket that pays.
4. **(REFUSED — measured tonight and the pre-registered question is now ANSWERED) The one-at-a-time index-ETF rule.** IMP-029's log (2026-08-10) parked this as *"genuinely suggested by today … but n=1; gather sessions first."* The sessions are gathered. **SPY/QQQ have been held concurrently exactly 4 times all-time** — 06-22 (−$37.74 combined), 07-09 (+$23.96), 08-10 (−$4.54), 08-26 (−$3.21) — for **−$21.53 total across 8 legs**, and **SPY is a net-POSITIVE symbol (n=12, +$21.52)** while QQQ is the loser (n=9, −$125.34). A guard that refuses the pair would refuse a profitable name to block an immaterial overlap, which is exactly the `bot/discriminator.py` collateral rule's refusal condition. **Question closed; do not re-propose.**
5. **⚠️ (ESCALATED, seventh run, and tonight adds the reason it must WAIT rather than the reason it is ignored) The flat `MIN_STOP_PCT` floor sets every stop and the ATR term is inert.** QQQ 1R = 1.48% vs a 1.18% ATR; **SPY 1R = 1.51% vs a 0.63% ATR — 2.4× the instrument's entire average daily range**; INTC runs 5.91%. Three instruments, one flat percentage. **New tonight, and it is the load-bearing point: `MIN_STOP_PCT` defines R, and R is the unit the entire ratchet is expressed in.** Changing the stop anchor would silently re-scale `BREAKEVEN_TRIGGER_R`, `TRAIL_TRIGGER_R` and `TRAIL_DISTANCE_R` on every instrument at once and **destroy IMP-040's open window**. So this is not merely "owned by the weekly" — it is **sequenced behind IMP-040's verdict**, and the weekly should say so explicitly rather than carrying it as an eighth unowned escalation.
6. **(REFUSED) Decoupling `TRAIL_DISTANCE_R` from `TRAIL_TRIGGER_R`** (08-25 item 4). Still the best-motivated response *if* the IMP-040 verdict is FAIL, and MSFT #292 adds a clean data point for it — the trail sat 0.25R = **0.34% of price** below the live, which is inside MSFT's 1-minute noise. **Not shippable at n=3 armed trades**, and shipping it would consume the very window that decides whether it is needed.
7. **(REFUSED) Any entry-layer change.** Fifteen discriminators are refuted and the standing rule is `bot/discriminator.py` era-controlled or nothing. Today gives no entry-layer evidence: MSFT never traded below its fill, and TSM/QQQ/SPY were all decided by the clock, not by the entry.

**Root-cause grouping.** There is nothing to root-cause on the loss side — **there were no losses**. On the win side the day splits cleanly: **(1) one genuine trend capture (INTC, +1.98R to its target) that survived a −0.774R excursion first**, **(2) one textbook ratchet scratch-to-profit (TSM, +0.40R)**, and **(3) one clock-decided drifter that happened to drift the right way (SPY, +0.27R over 6h19m)**. **Zero PLAN_STOP exits; no trade came within 22% of its 1R stop except INTC, which came within 23% of it and then ran.** ⚠️ **The honest attribution is that the tape did most of this** — see Market context.

### Market context
Perplexity `sonar` answered and the index-level read was usable: **2026-08-27 was a TRENDING, RISK-ON, tech-led session — S&P 500 +0.7% (7,730.99), Nasdaq +1.6%** — driven by chip/tech momentum off NVDA's after-hours beat (revenue $96.22B vs ~$92.2B consensus; the pre-market run had this). **Sonar was blind at the name level for both TSM and INTC** — the standing "regime lead only, never names" rule holds for a **fourth consecutive run**. ⚠️ **This matters for attribution and it cuts against reading today as evidence of edge:** the bot went 3W/0L long-only into a broad semis rally, and its two semiconductor names (INTC, TSM) supplied **$75.27 of $84.71**. **A long-only book on a +1.6% Nasdaq day should make money; the question the equity curve asks is what it does on the other days, and 25 sessions of answer say "loses".** The correct claim for today is narrow and worth making anyway: **the exit structure kept a materially larger share of what a good tape handed it than it would have three weeks ago** — INTC ran to its target instead of round-tripping, and TSM banked +0.40R instead of drifting to the clock.

### What worked / what didn't
**Worked**
- **The exit structure converted, for the first time in the IMP-040 era.** One TAKE_PROFIT (+1.98R) and one RATCHET_STOP (+0.40R) out of three trades. Era mix is now **RATCHET_STOP 4 (+$28.07) · TAKE_PROFIT 1 (+$60.84) · EOD_FLATTEN 6 (−$16.74) · PLAN_STOP 0**, net **+$72.17 over 11 trades**.
- **★ Zero PLAN_STOP exits in 11 IMP-040-era trades, against a post-gate baseline of 17.5% (14 of 80).** That bucket is the entire book loss (all-time: full-1R stops n=87, **−$4,854.69**, 1.1% win). Eleven trades cannot establish that it is fixed, but it is the pass direction and it is the one that matters most.
- **IMP-042's pre-registered 08-27 check PASSED cleanly.** Its text: *"no `ENTRY SKIPPED` line should name a symbol that appears in that day's `trades` table with an overlapping hold."* Today: **126 VWAP refusals across 7 symbols (TSLA×42, MSFT×36, QQQ×28, TSM×13, AAPL×4, CRM×2, NVDA×1)**, and **all 13 TSM refusals fall between 12:21:37 and 12:55:23 — after TSM's 10:35:21 exit.** **SPY (held 09:36→15:55) and INTC (held 09:56→10:30) generated zero refusals between them.** Under the old ordering SPY's 6h19m hold was precisely the shape that manufactured the 08-26 contamination. **Verdict: working as designed; the refusal count is now attributable.**

**Didn't**

Today: **TSM +0.219% adverse slip → TP sits at +1.181R (a 21% haircut on the planned reward)**, SPY **+1.443R**, **INTC −0.308% favourable slip → +1.974R (a 32% bonus, and it is the one that paid)**. ⚠️ **This is NOT a re-proposal of re-anchoring the stop to the fill — that was refused on 2026-08-25 and the refusal stands** (it would restore 1.5:1 by *tightening* the stop, reintroducing the 06-10 failure the 3×ATR/1.5% floor exists to prevent). Logged as a measured property, for the weekly. See Lessons 4.
