Post-gate the book is **87 trades, −$162.89**. **Today was a good draw from a negative distribution on a favourable tape.** The retire-or-rebuild decision escalated by the 08-22 weekly is still owed by a human, and a +1.1% day is not an answer to it.

### Lessons & improvement candidates
1. **★★ (REFUSED, and today makes the refusal easy rather than hard) Do not touch IMP-040's geometry.** Day 5 of a ~10-session pre-registration this log is bound by. Its three pass criteria are now all trending **PASS** and, for the first time, on ground truth rather than inference: **(a) EOD_FLATTEN share 54.5% vs the 58.8% post-gate baseline — falling**; **(b) "more `STOP RAISED` lines per trade" is now measurable at 3.7 per armed trade, 6 of 11 trades armed** (this was previously unmeasurable — see IMP-043); **(c) higher win rate with smaller average wins** — era win rate 6/11 = 54.5%. The FAIL signature (*"winners visibly cut short with no reduction in the give-back cohort"*) **did not fire today**: INTC ran to its target and TSM's give-back was exactly the configured trail distance. **Let it run to ~2026-09-08.**
2. **★★ SHIPPED as IMP-043 — the ratchet had no durable record and its evidence was about to expire.** `trades.stop_price` is pinned to the original 1R plan stop by design (IMP-013, it is the anchor that defines R), so **nothing in the schema recorded where the stop actually was when it fired** — a scratch and a full −1R loss both land in `exit_reason='STOP'`. The only record was `STOP RAISED` lines in a log that **logrotate keeps for 14 days**, while IMP-040's verdict is due ~09-08 — i.e. **the era's opening sessions would have rotated out before the verdict that depends on them.** TSM #297 is the concrete case: it exited at **425.33** on `exit_reason='STOP'` while `stop_price` still reads **415.20**, a **$10.13** gap that made a **+$14.43** ratchet scratch indistinguishable from a full −1R loss. Shipped two columns, the live writer, and a backfill that recovered **62 events across 16 trades with 0 unmatched** before they expired. Full detail in `memory/improvement-log.md`.
3. **(REFUSED — refuted, recorded so today does not revive it in reverse) The opening-window entry filter.** All three of today's entries were in the first 26 minutes and all three won, which is the mirror image of IMP-041's finding that 09:30–10:00 entries carry the entire post-gate loss. **Both readings are anecdote at this size.** The filter is refuted (IMP-016 time-of-day; and cutting that window removes 75% of the book — the "a rule whose optimum is *stop trading* is not a rule" trap this log has already named twice). **Do not propose it, and do not propose its inverse either.**
4. **★ (NEW, parked for the weekly with a registered trigger) The bracket anchors to the signal price while R is measured from the fill.** Evidence above: a 1.18R–1.97R realised-RR spread in one session from ±0.3% of slippage, and the adverse case is doubly bad (it widens real risk *and* shrinks real reward). ⚠️ **The obvious fix — re-anchor stop and TP to the fill — is already REFUSED (08-25) and that refusal stands.** The non-refused variant nobody has priced is **re-anchoring the TAKE-PROFIT only, leaving the stop exactly where it is** (no risk change, no 06-10 regression). It is **not shippable now**: it would move the TP on every trade while IMP-040's window is open, and the TP has fired **once** in the era. **Trigger: revisit at the IMP-040 verdict (~2026-09-08), together with item (a) of IMP-040's own deferral list ("the take-profit level … must be re-measured on post-IMP-040 data").** They are the same question and should be answered together.
5. **⚠️ (ESCALATED, eighth run — unchanged and still correctly sequenced) The flat `MIN_STOP_PCT` floor sets almost every stop.** Two of three today (SPY 1.4995%, TSM 1.4993%); INTC's 1.936% was the exception. **`MIN_STOP_PCT` defines R and R is the unit the entire ratchet is expressed in**, so moving it would silently re-scale all three IMP-040 constants at once and destroy the open experiment. **Owned by the weekly, sequenced AFTER ~2026-09-08.** Say it that way rather than carrying it as an unowned escalation.
6. **(REFUSED) Decoupling `TRAIL_DISTANCE_R` from `TRAIL_TRIGGER_R`.** Still the best-motivated response *if* the IMP-040 verdict is FAIL — and today weakens rather than strengthens it, since TSM's give-back was exactly the configured distance and INTC's ten raises tracked a real trend without being shaken out. **Not shippable while the window that decides whether it is needed is still open.**
7. **(REFUSED) Any entry-layer change, and any change reacting to INTC's −0.774R excursion.** Fifteen discriminators are refuted and the standing rule is `bot/discriminator.py` era-controlled or nothing. INTC drew down to within 33c of its stop and then made the day — **a filter that avoided that excursion would have avoided the day's profit**, which is the cleanest possible illustration of why per-trade excursion is not a signal-quality lever here.

**★★ The regime read and the bot's own equity curve agree to the quarter-hour, and together they explain the entire day.** Broker portfolio-history peaks at **11:00 ET (+$50.70)** and bleeds from there. **All four winners were entered BEFORE 10:00 ET (09:30, 09:36, 09:43, 09:55) and all four were taken out by ratchet stops during the roll-over.** **The single entry taken AFTER the 10:00 macro cluster — TSM at 10:02:32 — went straight to a full −1R.** That is not a story that needed a per-name catalyst, and sonar supplied none.
⚠️ **This is the exact scenario the 08-28 pre-market research pre-registered**, verbatim: *"the single most likely thing to turn a trending tape into a violent intraday reversal"*, with the note that *"the bot habitually has its full 3-slot book on before the 10:00 ET triple lands."* **It did, and that is why the day was green rather than red.** The pre-market call was correct and should be recorded as such.

**★★ RECONCILIATION — the "six-for-six, broker always LOWER" discrepancy is SOLVED, and it was never a bug.** Six pre-market runs have escalated an unexplained gap between DB `daily_summary.equity_close` and the broker's equity read the next morning ($0.91 → $0.25 → $0.21 → $0.21 → $0.21 → $0.16), each time noting the maddening detail that it *reconciles exactly at the close and reappears by morning*. Pulled `GET /v2/account/activities` (FEE) via the `alpaca` MCP this run. **It is regulatory fees, posted by Alpaca overnight at ~00:06Z (CAT) and ~00:17Z (TAF + REG) for the prior session:**

  | date | REG | TAF | CAT | total | gap the pre-market reported |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 08-27 | −$0.14 | −$0.01 | −$0.01 | **−$0.16** | **$0.16** ✓ |
  | 08-26 | −$0.19 | −$0.01 | −$0.01 | **−$0.21** | **$0.21** ✓ |
  | 08-25 | −$0.19 | −$0.01 | −$0.01 | **−$0.21** | **$0.21** ✓ |
  | 08-24 | −$0.19 | −$0.01 | −$0.01 | **−$0.21** | — |
  | 08-21 | −$0.23 | −$0.01 | −$0.01 | **−$0.25** | **$0.25** ✓ |

  | 08-19 | −$0.15 | −$0.01 | −$0.01 | −$0.17 | — |

  **Every retained day matches to the cent.** The REG (SEC Section 31) fee is **$20.60 per $1M of SELL proceeds, rounded UP to the cent** — that single rate reproduces all seven REG charges exactly ($6,382.23 → $0.14; $8,991.25 → $0.19; $11,150.82 → $0.23; …). TAF is $0.000166/share (floored at $0.01 here) and CAT is a flat $0.01/day. **`engine.py:547` reads `equity_close` from the broker at 16:00 ET, BEFORE the fees post — hence an exact match at the close and a gap by morning.** The bot self-corrects daily because `equity_open` is re-read from the broker at each open, so **no drift accumulates in the equity curve.** ⚠️ **Falsifiable prediction, pre-registered for the 08-29 pre-market read: today's sell proceeds were $12,947.78 over 48 shares, so REG = ceil($0.2667) = $0.27, TAF $0.01, CAT $0.01 → total −$0.29, and the broker should report equity ≈ $7,525.20 against a DB `equity_close` of $7,525.49.** **No code change: `trades.realized_pl` omits ~$0.05/trade of fees, ~$11 across 282 lifetime trades against a −$2,176 book — it changes no decision, and wiring a second broker call into the daily-summary path to capture it would cost more reliability than it buys.** **The question is CLOSED; do not re-escalate it.**

The gate refused CRM ten times and let exactly one entry through, at 09:43 while it was inside 0.25% of session VWAP — and that fill made +$10.96 on a 3-raise ratchet stop in 7m14s.** MFE +0.687R, MAE −0.184R. **Held with the plan stop only it LOSES −$2.84; the ratchet added +$13.80, the largest single contribution of the day.** ⚠️ **CRM is also the only name all week where `ATR_STOP_MULT = 3.0` bound (1.896% vs the 1.5% floor).** Strong, specific evidence for IMP-022 on precisely the case it was built for.
- **SPY #303 — a scratch that was worth $7.23.** Created 09:30:10, **filled 09:32:46 at 771.90** (see the latency note in Stats). It took **1h30m** to reach the 0.25R trigger, armed once at 11:00:41 to 772.06 (live 775.13, peak 774.97 — the trigger to the cent) and was taken out at 772.04 for **+$0.42 / +0.012R**. **MFE +0.276R** — it cleared the trigger by 0.026R and immediately rolled over with the tape. **Held to the flatten it loses −$6.81.** This is the ratchet doing exactly the job IMP-040 was shipped for: converting a give-back into a scratch.
- **MSFT #304 — the day's one ratchet cost, and it is a real one.** Filled 507.82, three raises in 4m22s (509.48 → 510.24 → 510.88) and out at 510.638 for **+$11.27 / +0.366R** after **14m52s**. **MFE over the hold was +0.691R; held with the plan stop only to the 15:55 flatten it makes +$24.84**, so **the ratchet cost −$13.57**. ⚠️ Note the mechanism: the first raise came at 09:42:58 on `live 511.40` — the trail armed and jumped **straight past break-even to 509.48**, 1.66 above entry, only six minutes in. **0.25R on MSFT is 0.375% of price, which is inside its 1-minute range**, so a name that kept trending all day was cut in fifteen minutes. This is the single strongest live instance of the "decouple `TRAIL_DISTANCE_R` from `TRAIL_TRIGGER_R`" candidate — and it is still not shippable tonight (Lessons 1).
- **WMT #308 — WMT's first fill in eleven sessions, and it is NOT a park.** Entered 12:01:56 after IMP-037 correctly cut 24 → 22 shares. **MFE +0.045R, MAE −0.344R**, drifted 3h53m35s to the clock for **−$4.41 / −0.130R**. ⚠️ **Its registered trigger is *"the next full-1R STOP parks it"* — this was an `EOD_FLATTEN` at −0.13R, not a stop of any kind. The trigger did NOT fire.** Say so explicitly to the pre-market rather than letting an eleven-session wait plus a small loss do the parking.

**Root-cause grouping.** Zero losses were caused by signal quality by the bot's own criteria, by a code bug, by bad bars, by re-entry, or by a risk-limit failure. **Zero exits came from a stale or missed stop; every bracket leg behaved.** The day splits into exactly three causes: **(1) one macro-timed full −1R (TSM, entered 2m32s after the 10:00 ET Warsh/payrolls cluster, MFE +0.030R) for −$31.65**; **(2) one structurally unresolvable low-ATR drifter (WMT, entire excursion 0.39R over 3h54m) for −$4.41**; and **(3) four pre-10:00 entries that the ratchet harvested into the roll-over for +$47.24.** **The ratchet is the only reason a session whose book peaked at 11:00 and bled for five hours closed green.**

### What worked / what didn't
**Worked**
- **★★ The ratchet earned money against "ratchet OFF" for the FIRST time in the IMP-040 era: +$11.75 today (helped 3 for +$25.32, hurt 1 for −$13.57).** The era ledger improves from **0 helped / 4 hurt / −$34.86** to **3 helped / 5 hurt / −$23.11**. **All three "helped" cases in the entire era happened today**, and the mechanism is legible: on a tape that peaked at 11:00 and faded, an early trail is worth exactly what a late one costs.
- **★★ IMP-043's pre-registered 08-28 check PASSED end-to-end, and against the broker rather than against the log it was recovered from.** Its text: *"any trade that arms tomorrow must show `stop_raises > 0` and a `final_stop_price` equal to the last `STOP RAISED` value in the log, written live by the engine with no backfill."* **Four armed trades, four live writes, zero backfill: SPY 1 → 772.06, MSFT 3 → 510.88, CRM 3 → 258.47, GOOG 7 → 343.75 — and all four `final_stop_price` values equal the `stop_price` Alpaca itself reports on those four bracket stop legs, to the cent.** TSM and WMT correctly read 0 / NULL. Row count went 16 trades / 62 raises → **20 / 76**, exactly +4 / +14, **and was unchanged by a full 471-test run** — IMP-043's `conftest.py` write-guard holds.
- **★ The VWAP gate bound on the most extended name the board has ever carried and let through only its one non-stretched moment** (CRM, 10 refusals / 1 fill / +$10.96). **27 refusals across 4 symbols (CRM×10, BAC×8, MSFT×5, INTC×4)**, and IMP-042's invariant holds again: **no `ENTRY SKIPPED` line names a symbol with an overlapping hold** — MSFT's 5 refusals are all after its 09:51 exit, and SPY/GOOG/TSM/WMT generated none while held.
- **Risk discipline held.** 0.41–0.48% risk/trade, halt never near (worst point of the day −0.617%), IMP-037 fired once and correctly, no naked-position alert, zero error lines, `NRestarts=0`, single-tick 15:55 flatten.
- **The pre-market's macro warning was precisely right and is the reason the review has a clean causal story.**

**Didn't**
- **★★ The one PLAN_STOP cost more than the four ratchet wins earned.** −$31.65 against +$47.24. **A 66.7% win rate produced +$11.18** because payoff is 0.655. **The bot's problem has never been win rate and today restates that in the cleanest possible form: it is the tail.**
- **★ Exit slippage is a material and previously unmeasured cost of the tightened ratchet: −$3.86 today, 34.5% of net.** See Stats. **Registered for the ~09-08 verdict** — a geometry that fires more stop-market exits pays this more often, and no existing tool prices it.
- **The `MIN_STOP_PCT` floor set 5 of 6 stops again.** WMT's 1R was 1.502% against a name whose whole daily range is ~1%; its entire 3h54m excursion was 0.39R. **Escalated for the ninth run and still correctly sequenced behind ~09-08** (Lessons 4).
- **The confidence scorer spanned 2.96 points across six trades (60.14–63.10) — its narrowest yet — and did not rank the day at all.** The best trade (GOOG, +$24.59) scored 61.57; the worst (TSM, −$31.65) scored 61.78. **It is not a scorer, it is a threshold.**
- **The strategy verdict is unchanged.** 26 sessions without a breakout-scored fill; all-time **282 closed trades, −$2,176.34**, `scripts.report` verdict **NEEDS WORK — expectancy not positive, false-breakout rate 52.7%**. **The retire-or-rebuild decision escalated by the 08-22 weekly is owed by a human and is now in its eighteenth session.** Three green days in four do not answer it.

### Lessons & improvement candidates
1. **★★ (REFUSED — day 7 of ~10, and today is the strongest evidence yet on BOTH sides) Do not touch IMP-040's geometry.** Its three pass criteria all read PASS on ground truth: **(a) `PLAN_STOP` share 1/17 = 5.9% against a 17.5% post-gate baseline**; **(b) 10/17 trades armed, 36 recorded stop raises, 3.6 per armed trade**; **(c) win rate 11/17 = 64.7% with smaller average wins.** Era net **+$83.35 over 17 trades**. ⚠️ **And the FAIL signature also fired once today** — MSFT was visibly cut short (−$13.57 vs holding, 0.25R = 0.375% of price, out in 14m52s while the name trended all day). **Both readings are live, the window has ~3 sessions to run, and consuming it now to chase a 17-trade sample is exactly the overfit this log is bound against. Let it run to ~2026-09-08.**
2. **★★ (NEW, and it is the reason tonight shipped IMP-044 rather than nothing) The instrument that will decide the ~09-08 verdict was scoring every candidate against the wrong baseline.** `scripts/exit_geometry.py` priced each what-if cell against the ACTUAL book, so every row inherited the simulator's own reproduction error. Tonight that error is **−$40.64** — the live geometry, replayed, sims **$42.71** against a book of **$83.35** — and it was silently subtracted from all twelve cells before they met a **$75.34** noise budget. Result: **every cell printed "⚠️ within noise"**, including cells whose real effect is +$79 to +$99. Shipped as IMP-044; details in `memory/improvement-log.md`. ⚠️ **What the debiased grid now says, recorded as pre-registered evidence FOR the 09-08 verdict and explicitly NOT acted on tonight: four `trail@1R-*` cells clear the noise budget (+$79.20 / +$87.85 / +$97.95 / +$98.75, each carried by 9 of 17 trades), and the whole `trail@0.5R-*` family beats the live cell (+$19.86 / +$37.50 / +$55.00).** **Every alternative in the grid beats the shipped 0.25R trail.** That is the direction MSFT #304 points, and it is the direction the "decouple `TRAIL_DISTANCE_R`" candidate has pointed for four runs. **It is still a 17-trade era and the window is still open. Read it on 09-08, not tonight.**
3. **(REFUSED — and stated carefully, because TSM #307 is genuinely tempting) A macro-event entry blackout.** TSM was entered 2m32s after the 10:00 ET Warsh/payrolls cluster and went straight to −1R; the pre-market predicted the mechanism in advance. **It is still refused, on three grounds.** **(a)** It requires an economic calendar the bot does not have and cannot verify, adding a live external dependency to the entry path for an event class that recurs a few times a month. **(b) n=1.** On 08-27 all three entries were inside the first 26 minutes and all three won; the mirror-image reading was refused then and must be refused now. **(c)** It is an entry-layer change, and the standing rule is `bot/discriminator.py` era-controlled or nothing — fifteen discriminators are refuted. **Recorded as a market-context fact, not a lever.**
4. **★ (REFUTED TONIGHT with fresh all-time numbers, recorded so it is never re-proposed) The "first entry of the day is taken on a vacuous VWAP gate" hypothesis.** SPY entered at **09:30:10**, ten seconds after the open, when session VWAP is computed off seconds of tape and therefore cannot refuse anything. Plausible — and **wrong**. All-time, bucketed by minutes after the open: **<1min n=25, 56.0% win, +$263.56, avg +$10.54 — the BEST bucket on the board.** (1–3min n=19, 26.3%, −$579.43; 6–10min n=32, 50.0%, −$63.92; 10–30min n=85, 43.5%, −$596.70; 30–60min n=38, 36.8%, −$445.25; 60min+ n=81, 32.1%, −$731.92.) **The opening print is where this bot makes its money. Do not filter it, and do not filter its inverse either.**
5. **⚠️ (ESCALATED, ninth run — unchanged, still correctly sequenced) The flat `MIN_STOP_PCT` floor sets almost every stop.** Five of six today; CRM's 1.896% was the lone ATR-bound exception. **WMT is the new textbook case: a 1.502% stop on a name whose entire session excursion was 0.39R.** `MIN_STOP_PCT` defines R and R is the unit all three ratchet constants are expressed in, so moving it re-scales IMP-040's experiment. **Owned by the weekly, sequenced AFTER ~2026-09-08.**
6. **★ (NEW, parked for the ~09-08 verdict with a registered trigger) Exit slippage is an unpriced cost of tightening the ratchet.** −$3.86 today across five stop-leg fills = **34.5% of net**, and per-share slip of 0.03–0.10% against a trail distance of 0.375% of price. **Neither `ratchet_audit.py` nor `exit_geometry.py` prices it** — the simulator fills at exactly the stop. **Trigger: measure it alongside the 09-08 verdict, together with the take-profit anchor (IMP-043 item b) and IMP-040's own deferred item (a).** All three are the same question: what the exit structure really nets after frictions.
7. **(REFUSED) Any reaction to the confidence scorer's 2.96-point span.** It has been flat for weeks, it is a threshold not a ranker, and re-scoring is an entry-layer change during an open exit-layer experiment. **Logged, not acted on.**
